\rightarrow
(iii)” follows from Theorem
5.1
.

(2) For the readers’ convenience, we first recall the description of
\mathcal{M}9
.
We start with a countable transitive model
M
of
\mathbf{ZFC}
. The notion of forcing is the set

\mathbb{P}=\{p\in\Fn(\omega\times\omega,\omega_{\omega}):(\forall\langle n,i\rangle\in\dom(p))(p(\langle n,i\rangle)\in\omega_{n})\}

endowed with the partial order of reverse inclusion. Let
G
be a
\mathbb{P}
-generic filter over
M
and let
M[G]
be the generic extension model of
M
. Then all cardinals smaller than
\omega_{\omega}^{M}
are collapsed to
\omega
in
M[G]
, since if
f=\bigcup G
, then
f
is a function (in
M[G]
) on
\omega\times\omega
, and, for every
n\in\omega
, the function
f_{n}
defined on
\omega
by
f_{n}(i)=f(n,i)
for all
i\in\omega
, maps
\omega
onto
\omega_{n}
(see Jech
[
14
, Example 15.57, p. 259]
).

Let
\mathcal{G}
be the group of all permutations
\pi
of
\omega\times\omega
such that

(\forall n\in\omega)(\forall i\in\omega)(\exists j\in\omega)(\pi(n,i)=\langle n,j\rangle).

Every
\pi\in\mathcal{G}
induces an automorphism of
\langle P,\leq\rangle
by

\displaystyle\dom(\pi p)

\displaystyle=\{\pi(n,i):\langle n,i\rangle\in\dom(p)\},

\displaystyle(\pi p)(\pi(n,i))

\displaystyle=p(n,i).

Let
\mathscr{F}
be the (normal) filter of subgroups of
\mathcal{G}
generated by
\{H_{n}:n\in\omega\}
, where

H_{n}=\{\pi\in\mathcal{G}:(\forall k\leq n)(\forall i\in\omega)(\pi(k,i)=\langle k,i\rangle)\}.

Model
\mathcal{M}9
is the symmetric submodel of
M[G]
which is determined by the group
\mathcal{G}
and the normal filter
\mathscr{F}
. That is,

\mathcal{M}9=\{\tau_{G}:\tau\in\HS\},

where
\HS
is the class of all hereditarily symmetric names in
M
and, for
\tau\in\HS
,
\tau_{G}
is the interpretation of
\tau
by
G
(see
[
13
, Chapter 5]
).

The following are known about
\mathcal{M}9
(see
[
14
, Example 15.57, p. 259]
):

(a)

For all
n\in\omega
,
f_{n}\in\mathcal{M}9
, so
\omega_{n}^{M}
is countable in
\mathcal{M}9
for all
n\in\omega
;

(b)

\omega_{\omega}^{M}
is countable in
M[G]
(recall that
M[G]
satisfies
\mathbf{AC}
and in
M[G]
,
\omega_{\omega}^{M}
is a countable union of countable sets);

(c)

\omega_{\omega}^{M}
is a cardinal in
\mathcal{M}9
;

(d)

\omega_{1}^{\mathcal{M}9}=\omega_{\omega}^{M}
(by (a) and (c)). So, in
\mathcal{M}9
,
\cf(\omega_{1}^{\mathcal{M}9})=\omega
.

We now show that
\mathbf{G}(\cf(\aleph)>\aleph_{0})
is true in
\mathcal{M}9
. First, note that
\mathbb{P}\subseteq\Fn(\omega\times\omega,\omega_{\omega})
. Consider the poset
\mathbb{S}=\langle\Fn(\omega\times\omega,\omega_{\omega}),\supseteq\rangle
. By
[
19
, Lemma 6.10, p. 213]
,
\mathbb{S}
has the
((\omega_{\omega})^{<\omega})^{+}
-cc property in
M
. Since
((\omega_{\omega})^{<\omega})^{+}=(\omega_{\omega})^{+}=\omega_{\omega+1}
, it follows that
\mathbb{S}
has the
\omega_{\omega+1}
-cc property in
M
. As
\mathbb{P}\subseteq\mathbb{S}
,
\mathbb{P}
has the
\omega_{\omega+1}
-cc property in
M
also. By
[
19
, Lemma 6.9, p. 213]
, we conclude that
\mathbb{P}
preserves cofinalities
\geq(\omega_{\omega+1})^{M}
and, since
(\omega_{\omega+1})^{M}
is regular,
\mathbb{P}
preserves cardinals
\geq(\omega_{\omega+1})^{M}
also. This, together with (b), gives us

\omega_{1}^{M[G]}=(\omega_{\omega+1})^{M}=\omega_{2}^{\mathcal{M}9}.

Thus, in
\mathcal{M}9
,
\cf(\omega_{2}^{\mathcal{M}9})>\omega
. This shows that (2) holds, as required.

(3) This follows from (1), (2), and the fact that
\omega_{1}^{\mathcal{M}9}
is singular in
\mathcal{M}9
(see
[
11
, pp. 153–154]
,
[
14
, Example 15.57, p. 259]
.
∎

Corollary 10.2
.

The following hold:

(1)

\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
is true in every permutation model of
\mathbf{ZFA}
.

(2)

There is a model of
\mathbf{ZF}
in which
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
is true and
\mathbb{R}
is a countable union of countable sets.

(3)

If
\mathbf{S}(\mathbb{R},[\mathbb{R}]^{\leq\omega})
is a
P
-space, then
\mathbb{R}
is not a countable union of countable sets, and the implication is not reversible in
\mathbf{ZF}
.

(4)

There is a model of
\mathbf{ZF}
in which
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
is true and
\mathbf{S}(\mathbb{R},[\mathbb{R}]^{\leq\omega})
is not a
P
-space.

(5)

\mathbf{PS}_{1}
implies
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
, but this implication is not reversible in
\mathbf{ZF}
.

(6)

\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
is true in the Basic Cohen Model
\mathcal{M}1
of
[
11
]
.

Proof.

(1) This follows from Proposition
4.18
and Corollary
8.8
.

(2) In the Feferman–Lévy Model
\mathcal{M}9
in
[
11
]
,
\mathbb{R}
is a countable union of countable sets (see
[
13
, Theorem 10.6]
). The conclusion now follows from Theorem
10.1
(2).

(3) The validity of the implication follows from Theorem
8.2
(iii). For the second assertion of (3), we note that, in
[
20
, Appendix]
, Arnold Miller constructed a variant of the Feferman–Lévy Model
\mathcal{M}9
, denoted by
V
, in which
\mathbb{R}=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}B_{n}
where, for every
n\in\omega
,
B_{n}
is a countable union of countable sets, but
\mathbb{R}
is not a countable union of countable sets in
V
. Thus, for some
n\in\omega
,
B_{n}
is uncountable in
V
, and so, by Theorem
3.6
(i),
\mathbf{S}(B_{n},[B_{n}]^{\leq\omega})
is not a
P
-space in
V
. This, together with Remark
3.10
and the fact that subspaces of
P
-spaces are
P
-spaces, yields
\mathbf{S}(\mathbb{R},[\mathbb{R}]^{\leq\omega})
is not a
P
-space in
V
.

(4) Since
\mathbb{R}
is a countable union of countable sets in the Feferman–Lévy Model
\mathcal{M}9
, it follows from (3) that
\mathbf{S}(\mathbb{R},[\mathbb{R}]^{\leq\omega})
is not a
P
-space in
\mathcal{M}9
. On the other hand, by Theorem
10.1
(2),
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
is true in
\mathcal{M}9
.

(5) It follows from Theorem
5.4
(also from Theorem
9.2
) that
\mathbf{PS}_{1}
implies the equality
\cf(\aleph_{1})=\aleph_{1}
. Thus, by Theorem
10.1
, (5) holds.

(6) This follows from Theorem
10.1
(1) and the fact that “
\cf(\aleph_{1})=\aleph_{1}
” is true in
\mathcal{M}1
(see
[
11
]
).
∎

Remark 10.3
.

(1)

We recall that Form 38 in
[
11
]
(i.e. “
\mathbb{R}
is not the union of a countable family of countable sets”) is strictly weaker than
\cf(\aleph_{1})=\aleph_{1}
in
\mathbf{ZF}
(see
[
13
, Problem 2, p. 148]
and
[
11
, Model
\mathcal{M}12(\aleph)
]
). In view of this observation, note in particular that Corollary
10.2
(2) is a
proper strengthening
of Theorem
10.1
(4).

(2)

By Theorem
10.1
(1) and the fact that
\mathbf{CUC}
(clearly) implies
\cf(\aleph_{1})=\aleph_{1}
, one readily obtains that
\mathbf{CUC}
implies
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
. It is worth noting here that a weaker union form also implies
\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)
. Indeed, Tachtsis
[
26
]
considered the following union form, denoted by
\mathbf{UT}(\aleph_{0},\cuc,\cuc)
in
[
26
]
: “If
\mathcal{A}
is a denumerable family of sets each of which can be expressed as a denumerable union of denumerable sets, then
\bigcup\mathcal{A}
can be expressed as a denumerable union of denumerable sets”.

In
[
26
, Theorem 5.2(1)]
, it was shown (among other results) that the principle
\mathbf{UT}(\aleph_{0},\cuc,\cuc)
implies
\mathbf{G}(\cf(\aleph)>\aleph_{0})
. Thus, by Theorem
10.1
(1),

\mathbf{UT}(\aleph_{0},\cuc,\cuc)\rightarrow\mathbf{P}(WO,0-dim,T_{2},crow)\rightarrow\mathbf{P}(0-dim,T_{2},crow).

On the other hand, by
[
26
, Theorem 5.7(1)]
,
\mathbf{UT}(\aleph_{0},\cuc,\cuc)
does not imply
\mathbf{CUC}
in
\mathbf{ZFA}
.

Appendix

Below, we give a crude proof of
\mathbf{PS}_{0}
in the Mostowski Linearly Ordered Model, using only the properties of the model rather than Theorem
6.4
. The proof is interesting in its own right and we include it because we consider it important to provide the readers with further insight and information.

Theorem.

\mathbf{PS}_{0}
is true in the Mostowski Linearly Ordered Model
\mathcal{N}3
of
[
11
]
.

Proof.

The description of
\mathcal{N}3
is as follows.
We start with a model
M
of
\mathbf{ZFA}+\mathbf{AC}
with a denumerable set
A
of atoms using an ordering
\leq
of
A
chosen so that
\langle A,\leq\rangle
is order-isomorphic to the set
\mathbb{Q}
of rational numbers with the usual ordering. Let
G
be the group of all order automorphisms of
\langle A,\leq\rangle
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be the (normal) filter of subgroups of
G
generated by the subgroups
\fix_{G}(E)
,
E\in[A]^{<\omega}
.
\mathcal{N}3
is the permutation model determined by
M
,
G
, and
\mathcal{F}
. If
x\in\mathcal{N}3
, then
\sym_{G}(x)\in\mathcal{F}
, so there exists
E\in[A]^{<\omega}
such that
\fix_{G}(E)\subseteq\sym_{G}(x)
. Under these circumstances, we call
E
a
support
of
x
.

Let
X
be an uncountable set in
\mathcal{N}3
. If
X
is well orderable in
\mathcal{N}3
, then, by Corollary
8.8
, we get that
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{\leq\omega})
is a
P
-space in
\mathcal{N}3
.

Suppose
X
is not well orderable in
\mathcal{N}3
. Let
E\in[A]^{<\omega}
be a support of
X
. By the proof of Theorem
9.9
(4), we may view
X
in
\mathcal{N}3
as a disjoint union
\bigcup\{X_{i}:i\in\gamma\}
, where
\gamma
is a well-ordered cardinal number, such that, for all
i\in\gamma
,
X_{i}\subseteq[A]^{<\omega}
. Since
A
is weakly Dedekind-finite in
\mathcal{N}3
and
\mathbf{UT}(\mathbf{WO},\mathbf{WO},\mathbf{WO})
is true in
\mathcal{N}3
(see
[
11
]
), it follows that
\{i\in\gamma:X_{i}
is infinite
\}\neq\emptyset
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
X_{i}
is infinite for all
i\in\gamma
. Moreover, we have

(15)

([X]^{\leq\omega}=[X]^{<\omega})^{\mathcal{N}3}\text{ and $([X]^{<\omega})^{\mathcal{N}3}$ is a $\sigma$-ideal.}

Thus,

(\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{\leq\omega}))^{\mathcal{N}3}=(\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega}))^{\mathcal{N}3}.

Furthermore,

(16)

(\forall\phi\in G)(\forall x,z\in[X]^{<\omega})(x\cap z=\emptyset\rightarrow\phi(B_{x,z})=B_{\phi(x),\phi(z)})

and

(\forall x,z\in[X]^{<\omega})(x\cap z=\emptyset\rightarrow(B_{x,z})^{M}=(B_{x,z})^{\mathcal{N}3})

since, by (
16
),
(\bigcup x)\cup(\bigcup z)
is a support of
B_{x,z}
.

To establish that
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
is a
P
-space in
\mathcal{N}3
, it suffices to show, by the second clause of (
15
) and by Theorem
3.6
(ii), that
\emptyset
is a
P
-point of
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
in
\mathcal{N}3
. To this end, let, in
\mathcal{N}3
,
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
be a
\subseteq
-decreasing sequence of open neighborhoods of
\emptyset
in
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
. Put

U=\bigcap_{n\in\omega}O_{n}.

If
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
has a finite range, then
U
is open in
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
. So we assume that the range of
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
is infinite and, without loss of generality, we further assume that
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
is strictly
\subseteq
-decreasing. Let
E^{\prime}\in[A]^{<\omega}
be a support of
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
; so
E^{\prime}
is a support of
O_{n}
for all
n\in\omega
. We can assume that
E\subseteq E^{\prime}
; otherwise, consider the set
E\cup E^{\prime}
which is a support of
\langle O_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
.
Note that
U\in\mathcal{N}3
, since
E^{\prime}
is a support of
U
. Indeed, if
\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})
, then we have

\phi(U)=\phi\left(\bigcap_{n\in\omega}O_{n}\right)=\bigcap_{n\in\omega}\phi(O_{n})=\bigcap_{n\in\omega}O_{n}=U.

In
M
(which satisfies
\mathbf{AC}
), pick an
\subseteq
-increasing sequence
\langle z_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
of elements of
[X]^{<\omega}
such that

(17)

(\forall n\in\omega)(B_{\emptyset,z_{n}}\subseteq O_{n}).

(Note that the sequence
\langle z_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
may not be in
\mathcal{N}3
, but, for every
n\in\omega
,
z_{n}\in\mathcal{N}3
.) Since
\langle z_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
is
\subseteq
-increasing, we clearly have

(18)

(\forall n\in\omega)(B_{\emptyset,z_{n+1}}\subseteq B_{\emptyset,z_{n}}).

If
\langle z_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
has a finite range, then it is straightforward to find (in
\mathcal{N}3
) a basic open neighborhood of
\emptyset
which is contained in
U
; in particular, take
B_{\emptyset,\bigcup_{n\in\omega}z_{n}}
. So we assume
\langle z_{n}\rangle_{n\in\omega}
is strictly
\subseteq
-increasing. It follows that, for all but finitely many
n\in\omega
,
(\bigcup z_{n})\setminus E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. Without loss of generality, we assume that

(\forall n\in\omega)\left(\left(\bigcup z_{n}\right)\setminus E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\right).

For every
n\in\omega
, we define

\displaystyle V_{n}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\big\{\phi(B_{\emptyset,z_{n}}):\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\big\{B_{\emptyset,\phi(z_{n})}:\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})\big\},

where the second equality follows from (
16
) and from the fact that
\phi(\emptyset)=\emptyset
. We observe the following:

(i)

For every
n\in\omega
,
V_{n}\in\mathcal{N}3
, since
E^{\prime}
is a support of
V_{n}
for all
n\in\omega
;

(ii)

for every
n\in\omega
,
V_{n}
is open in
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
, since it is a union of basic open sets in
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
;

(iii)

for every
n\in\omega
, we have

\emptyset\in V_{n+1}\subseteq V_{n}\subseteq O_{n}.

This follows from (
17
), (
18
), the definition of
V_{n}
(
n\in\omega
), and from the fact that
E^{\prime}
is a support of
O_{n}
for all
n\in\omega
.

We let

V=\bigcap_{n\in\omega}V_{n}.

By (i),
E^{\prime}
is a support of
V
, so
V\in\mathcal{N}3
(the argument is similar to the one for ‘
U\in\mathcal{N}3
’).
Furthermore, we have

(19)

\emptyset\in V\subseteq U.

Claim 11.14
.

In
\mathcal{N}3
, there exists
z\in[X]^{<\omega}
such that
B_{\emptyset,z}\subseteq V
.

Proof of claim.

Pick a
z\in[X]^{<\omega}\setminus\{\emptyset\}
such that
\mathcal{P}(E^{\prime})\cap X\subseteq z
.
We assert that
B_{\emptyset,z}\subseteq V
. To this end, let
w\in B_{\emptyset,z}
and also let
n\in\omega
. We will show that
w\in V_{n}
, that is, we will show that
w\in B_{\emptyset,\phi(z_{n})}
for some
\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})
(recall the definition of
V_{n}
). There are the following two cases.

Case 1.

w\cap z_{n}=\emptyset
. Then
w\in B_{\emptyset,z_{n}}\subseteq V_{n}
; the latter inclusion follows from the definition of
V_{n}
, taking
\phi
therein to be the identity permutation of
A
.

Case 2.

w\cap z_{n}\neq\emptyset
. Then, in view of the fact that
(\bigcup z_{n})\setminus E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, of the choice of
z
, and of the fact that
w\cap z=\emptyset
, it is not hard to construct a
\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})
such that

(20)

\phi(z_{n})\cap w=\emptyset.

Indeed, first note that, since
w\cap z=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}(E^{\prime})\cap X\subseteq z
, we have that, for every
u\in w\cap z_{n}
,
u\setminus E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. (And note that it is possible to have
(\mathcal{P}(E^{\prime})\setminus\{\emptyset\})\cap z_{n}\neq\emptyset
.) So now, since
z_{n}\subseteq[A]^{<\omega}
and
\langle A,\leq\rangle
is order-isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}
with the usual ordering, it is fairly easy to construct a
\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})
such that, for every
u\in z_{n}
with
u\setminus E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
,
\phi(u)\not\in w
. Note also that, for every
u\in\mathcal{P}(E^{\prime})\cap z_{n}
,
\phi(u)=u\not\in w
, due to the choices of
z
and
w
. But then, (
20
) holds as required.

Since
\phi\in\fix_{G}(E^{\prime})
, (
20
) together with the definition of
V_{n}
gives us

w\in B_{\emptyset,\phi(z_{n})}\subseteq V_{n}.

The above arguments complete case 2 and the proof of the claim.
∎

By (
19
) and Claim
11.14
, we deduce that
\emptyset
is a
P
-point of
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
in
\mathcal{N}3
. By Theorem
3.6
(ii),
\mathbf{S}(X,[X]^{<\omega})
is a
P
-space in
\mathcal{N}3
as required.
∎
